It's really not like me to be this scattered, so I figured I should come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with focus and organization your entire life, or is this a relatively new change for you?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not something I've dealt with my whole life. It's... it's a relatively new change. Um... my uncle died about two weeks ago. I think that's when this whole shift really started.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the mental fog, have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or how much you are sleeping since this began?\n\nPatient: My appetite has been okay, I guess, but my sleep has been all over the place. I just... I either lie there in bed thinking about all this family stuff, or I wake up way before my alarm goes off and I can't really settle back down to sleep. It's just been really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the feelings of guilt and the mental fog, have you been experiencing any physical sensations of anxiety, such as a racing heart, muscle tension, or feeling constantly on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My heart isn't racing or anything. I mean, I feel tired and maybe a little worn out from not sleeping well, but I wouldn't say my muscles are tense or that I feel constantly on edge physically. It's mostly just feeling really drained.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have been feeling more worried or fearful about the future than you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been worrying about the future or anything like that. It's more just... I'm stuck thinking about everything that just happened with my uncle. I'm not really fearful about what's ahead, just trying to get through right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you are still able to enjoy the activities or hobbies that you usually find pleasurable, or has your interest in those things decreased lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really lost interest in things. I mean... I still enjoy the stuff I usually do. Like I said, I'm pretty tired from not sleeping well, so maybe I don't always have the energy to do as much, but... yeah, when I actually sit down to watch a game or do something I like, I still get the same enjoyment out of it. That part hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the mistakes at work, have these feelings made it difficult for you to manage your usual responsibilities or social interactions with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has a little bit. Like at work, I’ve had to start asking my coworkers to double-check my client documentation for me just to be safe. And at home... my wife has actually taken over paying some of the bills and handling our scheduling. I'm just not as reliable with the details right now, so she's had to step in and help out with that stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your current level of distress or the difficulty you are having with your daily tasks is significantly greater than what most people would experience after the loss of an uncle?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to answer that. I mean... I guess it's hard to say what most people would go through. It's been tough for me, and yeah, I'm needing some extra help with things right now, but... I don't know if it's way worse than what someone else would feel. It just is what it is for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your reaction to your uncle's passing is interfering with your life to an extent that feels out of proportion to what you would typically expect from a grieving process?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to judge that, to be honest. I mean... losing someone close to you is a big deal, so I figured it makes sense that it's throwing me off my game for a bit. I don't have a lot of experience to compare it to, but... no, I wouldn't say it feels out of proportion. It just feels like grief.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this way, do you find yourself preoccupied with thoughts about your uncle, or are you mostly focused on your own inability to function as you normally do?\n\nPatient: I guess... I wouldn't say I'm focused on one more than the other. I'm just kind of dealing with all of it as it comes.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your symptoms are causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life that feels clearly beyond what you would expect from a normal grieving process?\n\nPatient: I still don't really know what counts as beyond normal grief. I'm just... It's just... the focus isn't there. It's... it's a relatively new change. It's just been really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the feelings of guilt and the mental fog, have you been experiencing any physical sensations of anxiety, such as a racing heart, muscle tension, or feeling constantly on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My heart isn't racing or anything. I mean, I feel tired and maybe a little worn out from not sleeping well, but I wouldn't say my muscles are tense or that I feel constantly on edge physically. It's mostly just feeling really drained.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have been feeling more worried or fearful about the future than you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been worrying about the future or anything like that. It's more just... I'm stuck thinking about everything that just happened with my uncle. I'm not really fearful about what's ahead, just trying to get through right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you are still able to enjoy the activities or hobbies that you usually find pleasurable, or has your interest in those things decreased lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really lost interest in things. I mean... I still enjoy the stuff I usually do. Like I said, I'm pretty tired from not sleeping well, so maybe I don't always have the energy to do as much, but... yeah, when I actually sit down to watch a game or do something I like, I still get the same enjoyment out of it. That part hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the mistakes at work, have these feelings made it difficult for you to manage your usual responsibilities or social interactions with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has a little bit. And at home... my wife has actually taken over paying some of the bills and handling our scheduling. I'm just not as reliable with the details right now, so she's had to step in and help out with that stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your current level of distress or the difficulty you are having with your daily tasks is significantly greater than what most people would experience after the loss of an uncle?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to answer that. I don't know if it's way worse than what someone else would feel.
